Narrator: NEEN-soon turned toward him. A faint smile touched her lips, but it did not reach her eyes.
Ninsun: [quietly] In this place, that is a dangerous assumption.
Narrator: She slid down from the wall and crossed to the dry fountain.
Narrator: NEEN-soon crouched beside its cracked basin, where a row of old chisel marks ran beneath the rim.
Ninsun: [testing] What do you see?
Narrator: BAH-lahg looked down.
Balag: [dryly] Something that needs water.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo lowered her knife.
Enhedu: [curious] Repairs.
Zik: Older than the courtyard,
Narrator: Zeek said.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon nodded.
Ninsun: [knowingly] The basin was moved here from somewhere else.
Narrator: She traced one of the marks without touching it.
Ninsun: [pointedly] The innkeeper says it has always been in this courtyard.
Zik: [dismissively] People say that about anything older than their grandparents,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Ninsun: [dryly] I asked his grandmother.
Narrator: BAH-lahg raised an eyebrow.
Ninsun: [pointedly] She said the same thing.
Balag: [dryly] That proves they're related.
Narrator: NEEN-soon ignored him.

Narrator: She pointed to a smooth section of stone.
Ninsun: [pointedly] There is no damage.
Zik: [skeptical] The edge could have been repaired,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Ninsun: [sharply] With stone from where?
Narrator: NEEN-soon took a folded scrap of parchment from her sleeve and opened it across the rim.
Narrator: A charcoal rubbing covered the page.
Narrator: Seven lines of cuneiform curved around the drawn outline of the basin.
Narrator: Zeek looked from the rubbing to the fountain.
Narrator: The stone beneath NEEN-soon's hand was blank.
Zik: [curious] What did it say?
Ninsun: An offering inscription.
Narrator: She held the parchment toward him.
Ninsun: [solemn] To the Lady Beneath, Keeper of the Seventh Door, that she may drink and remain below.
Narrator: BAH-lahg's hand tightened on his spear.
Balag: [alarmed] You copied that here?
Ninsun: [calmly] Three nights ago.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo came closer.
Enhedu: [uneasy] And now it's gone.
Ninsun: [pointedly] The cuts were deep enough to catch the charcoal.
Narrator: NEEN-soon pressed her thumbnail into the smooth stone.
Ninsun: [quietly] Now there isn't even a scar.
Narrator: Zeek took the rubbing.
Narrator: The marks were clear. Uneven in places where the paper had slipped, but clear enough that no one could mistake them for accidental scratches.
Zik: [sharply] Who else saw it?
Ninsun: The innkeeper.
Zik: [tense] Does he remember?
Ninsun: [dryly] He remembers me wasting charcoal on a blank fountain.
Narrator: BAH-lahg stood.
Balag: [uneasy] I don't like that.
Enhedu: [dryly] You don't like most things,
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo said.
Balag: [grumbling] I'm right about most things.
Narrator: Zeek read the inscription again.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The Lady Beneath.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The Seventh Door.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Remain below.
Narrator: His father's message shifted inside his satchel with the movement of his breath.
Zik: [quietly] It erased the stone,
Narrator: he said.
Narrator: NEEN-soon looked toward the buildings beyond the courtyard.
Ninsun: [darkly] Or replaced it.
Zik: [tense] With what?
Ninsun: [quietly] The fountain everyone remembers.
Narrator: For the first time, BAH-lahg had no quick answer.
Narrator: A pigeon landed on the courtyard wall. It took two steps, stopped, and cocked its head toward an empty doorway at the far end.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Zeek frowned.
Narrator: There had not been a doorway there when he entered.
Narrator: He was certain of it.
Narrator: The opening was narrow, framed by mud brick darkened with age. A passage ran beyond it for several paces before bending out of sight.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo followed his gaze.
Enhedu: [alarmed] That was a wall.
Narrator: BAH-lahg turned.
Balag: [tense] It still should be.
Narrator: NEEN-soon did not look surprised.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: She looked tired.
Ninsun: [tired] The fog isn't thick enough.
Narrator: BAH-lahg gripped his spear.
Balag: [tense] What does that mean?
Ninsun: [tired] It usually hides the change.
Narrator: The pigeon stepped into the passage.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Halfway through, it vanished.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not flew.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not turned.
Narrator: Vanished between one pace and the next.
Narrator: BAH-lahg rose with his spear in both hands.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo moved away from the pillar, knife low at her side.
Narrator: Zeek crossed the courtyard until he stood three strides from the opening. The bricks around it looked old. More than old. Worn smooth at shoulder height by generations of people passing through.
Narrator: A line of pale clay footprints marked the floor inside.
Narrator: They entered from the courtyard.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: None came back.
Narrator: NEEN-soon's voice came quietly behind him.
Ninsun: [softly] My sister walked into a district that shouldn't have existed three years ago.
Narrator: Zeek did not take his eyes from the passage.
Ninsun: [quietly] The streets rearranged themselves around her. When the fog lifted, she was gone. The city records never listed her. No one else remembers her name except me.
Narrator: The courtyard went still.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo's knife stopped moving.
Narrator: BAH-lahg looked from the wall to NEEN-soon.
Narrator: Her voice stayed level.
Ninsun: [intense] I've spent every year since then in the lower archives trying to prove the old texts were not metaphor. The Seven Gates. The lid over the Great Below. The queen who never leaves her throne. I thought if I could map the changes, I might find where the City took her.
Narrator: She met Zeek's eyes.
Ninsun: [intense] Your mark is the same symbol that appears on the oldest underworld seals. The ones no living scribe will copy.
Narrator: The doorway shuddered.
Narrator: For one instant the passage beyond appeared flooded with black water.
Narrator: Then it was only shadow again.
Narrator: Zeek tasted the dream at the back of his throat.
Narrator: The impossible doorway began to narrow.
Narrator: The old bricks slid toward one another without sound.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Zeek stepped forward.
Narrator: BAH-lahg caught the back of his tunic.
Balag: [firmly] No.
Zik: [tense] There are prints.
Balag: [firmly] There are always prints.
Zik: [quietly] Not like these.
Balag: [dryly] Do the prints carry spears?
Narrator: Zeek looked at him.
Narrator: BAH-lahg tightened his grip.
Balag: [firmly] Then they can wait.
Narrator: The gap narrowed to the width of a man's shoulders.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo stepped closer, studying the clay footprints.
Enhedu: [tense] They're fresh.
Narrator: NEEN-soon's face changed.
Ninsun: [urgently] How fresh?
Enhedu: [uneasy] The edges haven't dried.
Narrator: The nearest print darkened.
Narrator: Water gathered inside it.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Black water.
Narrator: NEEN-soon took one step toward the passage.
Narrator: Zeek caught her wrist.
Narrator: She tried to pull free.
Ninsun: [desperate] My sister—
Zik: [firmly] Is not a pigeon.
Ninsun: [desperate] You don't know that she isn't in there.
Zik: [tense] I don't know what 'there' is.
Narrator: The passage narrowed again.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon's voice sharpened.
Ninsun: [sharply] Let go.
Zik: [firmly] Not until the wall closes.
Ninsun: [pleading] That could be the only opening we get.
Zik: [firmly] Then we get another.
Ninsun: [desperate] You don't know that.
Zik: [quietly] No.
Narrator: Zeek looked at the wet footprints disappearing into the dark.
Zik: [grimly] But whatever made this wants us to hurry.
Narrator: The bricks moved closer.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon stopped pulling.
Narrator: The last sliver of passage showed only blackness.
Narrator: Something shifted inside it.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not a person.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Too tall.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Too narrow.
Narrator: Then the bricks met.
Narrator: The wall sealed itself.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: No seam remained.

Narrator: NEEN-soon looked down at Zeek's hand around her wrist.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: He released her.
Narrator: She rubbed the pale marks his fingers had left.
Ninsun: [knowingly] The ink is still wet, isn't it?
Narrator: she asked.
Narrator: Zeek felt the journal's weight inside his satchel.
Zik: [suspicious] I didn't tell you about the new writing.
Ninsun: [quietly] I know.
Narrator: Her gaze dropped to his wrapped hand.
Ninsun: [ominous] The old tablets say the First City was never a place of the living. It was built as a seal. A lid. Every so often the seal weakens, and something underneath begins to notice the people walking above it.
Narrator: Zeek closed his hand around the satchel strap.
Zik: [firmly] We leave before the fog lifts again.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: No one argued.
Narrator: He had not told them about the final mark the journal had drawn.
Narrator: He had not told them that the message used the name his father only spoke when frightened, or when trying to make certain his son was listening.
Narrator: He would tell them later.
[pause 1.2s]
Narrator: Or the City would tell them first.
[pause 3s]


CHAPTER 8 — FOUR WORKING

Narrator: CHAPTER 8
[pause 1s]
Narrator: Four Working
[pause 2s]
Narrator: The first shifted street they found together was almost polite about it.
Narrator: It began as an ordinary alley behind a row of dye-works in the lower western district. The morning air there tasted of copper, vinegar, and wet wool. Strips of dyed cloth hung from upper windows, dripping red and indigo onto the packed earth below. Workers moved between vats with their arms stained to the elbow, shouting measurements at one another over the hiss of boiling water.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: By the time Zeek and the others reached the middle of the alley, the noise had thinned.
Narrator: The walls had grown older.
Narrator: The bricks darkened from sun-baked brown to a nearly black clay that held a faint sheen beneath the grime. The air cooled against Zeek's face, carrying the mineral smell of sealed stone and standing water. Behind them, a dye-worker cursed as someone spilled a vat.
Narrator: The voice sounded much farther away than it should have.
Narrator: Where the alley ought to have ended against the back of a warehouse, it simply continued—a narrow descending passage that none of the city maps recorded and none of the locals would admit existed when asked.
Narrator: BAH-lahg stopped at the change in the masonry and tested the ground with the butt of his spear.
Balag: [uneasy] This wasn't here yesterday.
Enhedu: [dryly] It wasn't here when we turned the corner,
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo said.
Narrator: She was already examining the join where new brick met old. The seam did not look built. It looked grown. One wall belonged to a warehouse raised within living memory. The other had been worn smooth by hands that had not touched it in centuries.
Narrator: NEEN-soon had her stylus out before the rest of them finished speaking.
Narrator: She pressed a damp clay tablet against her forearm and began copying the seam with the focused intensity of someone who had waited years for the city to make a mistake she could catch in writing.
Narrator: BAH-lahg glanced at the tablet.
Balag: [teasing] You planning to arrest it?
Ninsun: [primly] I'm documenting it.
Balag: [teasing] So we can arrest it later.
Ninsun: [firmly] So when the alley disappears, one of us possesses proof it existed.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo leaned closer to the bricks.
Enhedu: [thoughtful] Clay changes.
Ninsun: [firmly] Not without leaving a mark.
Enhedu: [dryly] This city seems fond of exceptions.
Narrator: NEEN-soon did not look up.
Ninsun: [firmly] Then I'll document the exception.
Narrator: Zeek stood back and watched the three of them settle into the work without discussion.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg checked structure.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo checked value.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon checked history.
Narrator: The division of labor had formed in less than a day and no one had needed to declare it.
Narrator: Zeek checked the exits.
Narrator: Behind them, the alley remained open. A blue length of cloth stirred above the entrance. Beyond it, people moved across the ordinary street without looking in. One man stopped near the mouth of the alley, adjusted a jar against his hip, and continued walking as if four people had not just vanished into a passage where no passage belonged.
Narrator: Zeek took a length of chalk from his pouch and drew a short line across the bricks at shoulder height.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo watched him.
Enhedu: [curious] So we know whether the wall moves?
Zik: [dryly] So we know whether we do.
Narrator: BAH-lahg gave the dark passage another look.
Balag: [grumbling] That sentence made things worse.
Zik: [dryly] It wasn't meant to help.
Narrator: They moved in the order that was already becoming habit: BAH-lahg first, En-KHEH-doo second, NEEN-soon third, Zeek last.
Narrator: The walls narrowed until shoulders brushed stone. The temperature dropped with every dozen steps. Somewhere ahead water dripped with the slow patience of a place that had never needed to hurry.
Narrator: Zeek counted the steps.
Narrator: Twelve to the first turn.
Narrator: Nine to the second.
Narrator: Then fourteen down a slope so gradual he felt it more in his knees than saw it beneath his feet.
Narrator: At the twenty-seventh step, the noise of the dye-works disappeared completely.
Narrator: BAH-lahg raised a hand.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Everyone stopped.
Narrator: A long horizontal crack ran through three courses of the left wall. Not fresh. Not old enough to ignore.
Narrator: BAH-lahg pressed the end of his spear against the lowest brick and leaned.
Narrator: Dust whispered from the seam.
Balag: [tense] Load's shifted from above,
Narrator: he said.
Narrator: NEEN-soon spoke before Zeek could move.
Ninsun: [knowingly] The mortar's different there. See the color? That's not the same mix as the rest of the wall. Someone patched it once already and did a bad job.
Narrator: Zeek looked at her, then at the crack.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: She was right.
Narrator: The repair was older than the settling, and the original workman had used a weaker lime. Worse, the patch had been laid over soot.
Narrator: He scraped at the edge with his awl.
Narrator: A thin black layer came away.
Zik: [quietly] This wall burned,
Narrator: he said.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo turned from the passage ahead.
Enhedu: [curious] Recently?
Zik: No.
Enhedu: How long?
Zik: [thoughtful] Long enough for someone to rebuild over it. Not long enough for the soot to wash out of the joints.
Narrator: BAH-lahg looked up at the low ceiling.
Balag: [uneasy] What burned underground?
Zik: [dryly] Something with air.
Balag: [sarcastic] That narrows it down.
Narrator: Zeek set his pack against the opposite wall and removed a short timber, a wedge, and a coil of cord. The timber had been cut for tent work, not masonry, but the grain ran straight and the wood was dry.
Narrator: He positioned it beneath the weakest section.
Narrator: BAH-lahg held the upper end while Zeek drove the wedge.
Narrator: The wall gave a low grinding sound.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Everyone went still.
Narrator: The crack widened by the thickness of a fingernail.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Then stopped.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg looked down.

Zik: [dryly] Still deciding.
Balag: [grumbling] Decide away from me.
Narrator: Zeek adjusted the angle, drove the wedge again, and tested the pressure until the crack stopped complaining.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Temporary.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Sufficient.
Narrator: NEEN-soon watched him finish.
Ninsun: [curious] You do that a lot.

Ninsun: [quietly] Yes.

Narrator: BAH-lahg snorted.

Narrator: NEEN-soon touched the repaired mortar with the end of her stylus.

Zik: [dryly] I noticed you noticed the mortar.
Ninsun: [teasing] That sounded almost like praise.
Zik: [flatly] It wasn't.
Ninsun: [dryly] Good. I'd hate to misrecord it.
Narrator: They kept going.
Narrator: The passage widened just enough for BAH-lahg to lower his spear. The floor changed beneath their boots. Packed earth became fitted stone, each slab cut in a long narrow rectangle and laid with a precision the city above no longer bothered to imitate.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo crouched.
Enhedu: [alert] There are marks here.
Narrator: Zeek joined her.
Narrator: At first he saw only scratches. Then the pattern resolved.
Narrator: Parallel grooves ran along the edges of the slabs, shallow but regular.

Narrator: BAH-lahg said.
Zik: [thoughtful] Too even,
Narrator: Zeek answered.
Narrator: NEEN-soon lowered the lamp.
Narrator: The light caught a second set of grooves crossing the first.
Ninsun: [curious] A cart route?
Enhedu: [skeptical] Underground?
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo asked.
Narrator: Zeek ran two fingers through the nearest channel.
Zik: [thoughtful] No wheel hubs. No hoof cuts.
Narrator: He looked along the passage.
Zik: [uneasy] Something heavy was moved through here repeatedly.
Narrator: BAH-lahg's gaze went toward the darkness ahead.
Balag: [uneasy] Toward us or away?
Zik: [dryly] The grooves don't say.
Balag: [grumbling] They should learn.
Narrator: NEEN-soon copied the pattern onto her tablet.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo stood and dusted her hands.
Enhedu: [thoughtful] Maybe the stone was quarried somewhere below and brought up.
Zik: Then the edges would be chipped,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Zik: They aren't.
Enhedu: [curious] What was moved?
Narrator: Zeek looked down at the grooves.
Zik: [ominous] Something the builders expected to move again.
Narrator: A drop of water struck the floor ahead.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then another.
Narrator: The sound did not echo correctly.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: It returned twice.
Narrator: Once from the passage.
Narrator: Once from somewhere beneath them.
Narrator: BAH-lahg heard it too. His grip shifted on the spear.

Narrator: The mark beneath the linen warmed at once.
Narrator: He pulled his hand away.

Narrator: Solid.

Narrator: The passage ended in a small square chamber that should not have existed.
Narrator: Its proportions were wrong for storage and too cramped for worship. A single doorway had been blocked with bricks of several different ages. The faded remains of a painted boundary stone had been set into the floor, its upper surface worn nearly smooth.
Narrator: NEEN-soon went straight to the stone.

[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: No workers.

Narrator: No carts.

Narrator: NEEN-soon moved quickly. She placed a thin sheet of damp parchment across the painted stone and rubbed charcoal over the surface.

Narrator: Not writing.

CHAPTER 9 — THE NAME WE LEFT

Narrator: En-KHEH-doo pulled him through the narrowing gap.
Narrator: They stumbled into the street together.
Narrator: The water-seller was still shouting.
Water-Seller: [indignant] I measured it in front of you.
Water Customer: [sarcastic] With your thumb in the cup.
Water-Seller: [indignant] My thumb is not that large.
Water Customer: [sarcastic] It has always been that large.
Narrator: Behind them, the dark opening narrowed.
Narrator: NEEN-soon turned toward it.
Narrator: The chamber was no longer visible. Only a strip of black passage remained.
Narrator: A voice came through.
The City: [whispers] Ahm-MAH-roo.
Narrator: NEEN-soon took a step forward.
Narrator: BAH-lahg blocked her with the shaft of his spear.
Balag: [firmly] No.
Narrator: The passage repeated the name.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then another.
The City: [whispers] NEEN-soon.
Narrator: Her face went pale.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The opening closed.
Narrator: Ordinary brick filled the wall.
Narrator: The water-seller's customer shoved a cup into his chest and stalked away.
Narrator: No one else looked at the four of them.
Narrator: NEEN-soon opened her satchel.
Narrator: The scrap of parchment was damp.
Narrator: The name remained in her handwriting.
Narrator: Beneath it, a second line had appeared.
Narrator: The ink was not hers.
Narrator: [slowly] A NAME SPOKEN IS A DOOR LEFT OPEN.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo read over her shoulder.
Enhedu: [dryly] That sounds less like advice than a threat.
Narrator: NEEN-soon folded the parchment carefully.
Ninsun: [quietly] You asked whether I would tell anyone.
Enhedu: [flatly] I did.
Ninsun: [firmly] Not yet.
Enhedu: [curious] Why not?
Ninsun: [bitterly] Because the moment I do, someone with a better title will decide it belongs in a locked drawer.
Narrator: She tucked the parchment deeper into the satchel.
Ninsun: [firmly] I'd rather it stay on a piece of paper I control.
Narrator: BAH-lahg grunted.
Balag: [grunts] That's either smart or the kind of thinking that gets people buried under better titles.
Ninsun: [dryly] Probably both.
Narrator: They stepped fully back into the ordinary flow of the street.
Narrator: The dark mouth in the wall had become a shallow recess. A basket seller had already set three empty crates against it. Any local would have sworn the recess had always been there.
Narrator: Zeek flexed his left hand inside the wet linen.
Narrator: The mark stayed quiet.
Narrator: NEEN-soon walked ahead, one hand resting over her satchel.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Zeek watched her.
Narrator: He considered telling her about the second name on the basin.
Narrator: He considered how quickly the chamber had learned her voice.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: He said nothing.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: They walked on.
Narrator: Behind them, in a room the city no longer admitted existed, one name remained uncrossed.
[pause 1.2s]
Narrator: And beside it, another waited.
[pause 3s]


CHAPTER 10 — THE PASSAGE WE MARKED

Narrator: CHAPTER 10
[pause 1s]
Narrator: The Passage We Marked
[pause 2s]
Narrator: They started marking the shifted streets on the fifth day.
Narrator: It wasn't NEEN-soon's idea, though she was the one who made it permanent. After the second uncrossed name and a third alley that closed behind them before they finished measuring it, BAH-lahg simply said,
Balag: [annoyed] I'm tired of walking into the same fucking hole twice,
Narrator: and that settled the argument.
Narrator: They used chalk at first. White marks on the older brick where a passage had opened. Simple. Visible. Temporary.
Narrator: By the second afternoon the chalk marks were gone.
Narrator: Not washed off. Not worn away. Simply absent, as if the wall had never accepted them.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo crouched where the latest mark should have been and ran her thumb across the brick.
Enhedu: [uneasy] It's eating the chalk.
Ninsun: [thoughtful] Or deciding the chalk doesn't belong,
Narrator: NEEN-soon said. She was already pulling a thin bronze stylus and a scrap of harder stone from her satchel.
Ninsun: [firmly] We need something it can't ignore as easily.
Narrator: Zeek watched her cut a small, precise notch at the base of the wall, low enough that most people would miss it, deep enough that weather alone wouldn't erase it in a day. The sound of the stylus on brick was sharp and final.
Narrator: BAH-lahg tested the notch with his boot.
Balag: [grumbling] That'll do until it doesn't.
Zik: [dryly] Same as everything else,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Narrator: They kept the system simple after that. One notch for a passage that had opened and closed. Two notches if it had shown names or objects worth remembering.
Narrator: Three if it had tried to keep them. No one wrote explanations. Explanations were for people who expected the walls to stay still long enough to read them.
Narrator: On the sixth day they found a passage that already carried two notches they didn't remember cutting.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: No one spoke for a while.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo was the first to break the silence.
Enhedu: [uneasy] Either we're forgetting our own marks, or someone else is using the same system.
Ninsun: [softly] Or the city is,
Narrator: NEEN-soon said quietly.
Narrator: BAH-lahg spat into the dust.
Balag: [disgusted] I hate every version of that sentence.
Narrator: Zeek ran his fingers over the two clean notches. The edges were sharp. Recent. The same depth and angle NEEN-soon used. He looked at her. She shook her head once. Not hers.
Narrator: NEEN-soon crouched beside the marks.
Ninsun: [sharply] Give me the lamp.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo lowered it until the flame threw the notches into sharp relief. NEEN-soon took the bronze stylus from her satchel and held its tip against the first cut without touching the brick.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The angles matched.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not approximately.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Exactly.
Narrator: BAH-lahg leaned over her shoulder.
Balag: [suspicious] You said they weren't yours.
Ninsun: [firmly] They aren't.
Balag: [skeptical] That tool disagrees.
Narrator: NEEN-soon rotated the stylus.
Narrator: A small chip marred one edge of its point. Zeek had noticed it the day before when she marked the passage with the basin. The flaw left a narrow ridge in every cut she made, faint enough to miss unless the light struck from the side.
Narrator: NEEN-soon moved the lamp.
Narrator: A thin line appeared inside the first notch.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Then the second.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The same ridge.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo looked at the stylus.
Enhedu: [curious] How many of those are there?
Ninsun: [quietly] This one.
Enhedu: [curious] You bought it recently?
Ninsun: [softly] My sister gave it to me.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: No one spoke.
Narrator: NEEN-soon pressed her thumb against the chipped edge.
Ninsun: [softly] I have carried it for six years.
Narrator: BAH-lahg straightened.
Balag: [skeptical] So either you cut those and forgot—
Ninsun: [firmly] I didn't.
Balag: [skeptical] —or someone borrowed your stylus.
Ninsun: [defensive] It has not left my satchel.
Balag: [dryly] Things leave your satchel.
Ninsun: [firmly] Not without opening it.
Balag: [uneasy] In this city, I'm no longer certain that matters.
Narrator: Zeek examined the ground.
Narrator: No footprints marked the dust inside the passage. No disturbed grit. No scrape where someone had braced a hand against the wall to make the cuts.
Narrator: He looked at the second notch.
Narrator: The lower end curved slightly to the left.
Narrator: NEEN-soon always did that when cutting downward. Her wrist turned inward near the end of the stroke.
Narrator: He had watched her make the same mistake eleven times.
Zik: [quietly] These are yours,
Narrator: he said.
Narrator: NEEN-soon looked up sharply.
Ninsun: [sharply] I told you—
Zik: [gently] I didn't say you remember making them.
Narrator: The passage breathed cold air over them.
Narrator: Somewhere inside, stone clicked against stone.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Once.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then twice.
Narrator: BAH-lahg lowered his spear toward the opening.
Balag: [uneasy] It knows the system.
Enhedu: [quietly] Or we come back here,
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo said.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon stood.
Ninsun: [uneasy] We haven't been here before.
Enhedu: [ominous] Not yet.
Narrator: BAH-lahg looked at her.
Balag: [uneasy] I liked you better before you started saying things like that.
Narrator: A shape moved at the far end of the passage.
Narrator: Not close enough to identify.
Narrator: Close enough that all four of them saw it.
Narrator: It stood with one arm raised against the wall.
Narrator: A small sharp sound reached them.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Bronze cutting brick.
Narrator: NEEN-soon's hand closed around her stylus.
Narrator: The figure made one stroke.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then another.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Two notches.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: The passage darkened.
Narrator: The figure was gone.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg stepped backward.
Balag: [whispers] [tense] No.

Zik: [quietly] We could follow.

Narrator: NEEN-soon said.

Balag: [impatient] You make decisions. Make one.

Narrator: Not pulling him forward.
Narrator: Not warning him away.

Narrator: Waiting.
Narrator: He thought of the chamber that had learned NEEN-soon's name. The black water bending toward his father's journal. The city producing evidence faster than they could understand it.
Zik: [firmly] Three notches,
Narrator: he said.

Ninsun: [surprised] We haven't entered.
Zik: [grimly] No. The third one is for a passage that knows us.
Narrator: She placed the stylus against the brick.
Narrator: For the first time since he had met her, her hand trembled.
Narrator: The cut came out straight anyway.
Narrator: They walked away without entering.
Narrator: They did not enter that passage.
Narrator: They cut a third notch beside the two, deeper than the others, and walked away.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: That night, back in the cheap room they were using as a base, NEEN-soon spread her scraps of parchment on the floor and began transferring every notch location she could still remember onto a single sheet. The map was ugly, incomplete, and already out of date in places.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: She worked anyway.
Narrator: Zeek watched from the doorway.
Zik: [dryly] You know it's going to change again the moment we trust it,
Narrator: he said.
Ninsun: [quietly] I know.
Zik: [curious] Then why keep it?
Narrator: NEEN-soon didn't look up.
Ninsun: [firmly] Because the alternative is letting the city be the only one writing anything down.
Narrator: She drew another line.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Then stopped.
Narrator: Zeek remained in the doorway.
Zik: [curious] What?
Ninsun: [evasive] Nothing.
Zik: [pointedly] You stopped.
Ninsun: [defensive] That doesn't mean something happened.
Zik: [dryly] With you, it usually does.
Narrator: NEEN-soon shifted three scraps of parchment across the floor. Each held a rough section of street, copied at a different scale. None aligned cleanly with the others.
Narrator: She placed a clay cup on one corner to keep the papers flat.
Ninsun: [sharply] Hold that lamp lower.
Narrator: Zeek entered and crouched beside her.
Narrator: The room smelled of lamp smoke, old plaster, and the sour beer BAH-lahg had abandoned near the window. NEEN-soon had marked every shifted passage with a short black line. Beside each she had recorded the number of notches and the day it appeared.
Narrator: Zeek studied the scattered marks.
Zik: [thoughtful] They're moving west.
Ninsun: [quietly] They were.
Zik: Were?
Ninsun: [intently] Yesterday they moved south.
Narrator: She rotated one sheet.
Ninsun: [intently] Today they moved back toward the flood channel.
Zik: [skeptical] You think there's a pattern.
Ninsun: [dryly] I think patterns are what people call confusion when they want it to feel temporary.
Narrator: Zeek adjusted the lamp.
Narrator: The shadows of the parchment edges shifted.
Narrator: Several marks lined up.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not streets.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not a route.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: A curve.
Narrator: NEEN-soon saw it at the same moment.
Narrator: She pulled another scrap beneath the first.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then another.
Narrator: The separate curves joined.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: A spiral.
Narrator: Zeek's wrapped hand tightened.
Narrator: NEEN-soon noticed but did not look at it.
Ninsun: [tense] The passages aren't appearing randomly,
Narrator: she said.
Zik: [curious] What are they doing?
Narrator: She drew the final arc in charcoal.
Narrator: The line curled inward toward a point near the center of the lower districts.
Narrator: The old flood channel.
Zik: [alarmed] No,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Narrator: NEEN-soon measured the distances again.
Ninsun: [ominous] They're tightening.
Zik: [uneasy] Around what?
Ninsun: [quietly] I don't know.
Zik: [pointedly] You know where.
Ninsun: [defensive] That is not the same thing.
Narrator: She placed the tip of her stylus at the spiral's center.
Narrator: The floor beneath them gave a faint knock.
Narrator: Both of them froze.
Narrator: A second knock came from directly below the map.
Narrator: NEEN-soon lifted the parchment.
Narrator: The floor was bare.
Narrator: When she set it down again, a new mark had appeared at the center of the spiral.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Three notches.
Narrator: Zeek took the lamp from her and held it closer.
Narrator: The ink was wet.
Narrator: Neither of them had drawn it.

Zik: [sharply] Don't.

Ninsun: [curious] Why?

Narrator: NEEN-soon looked back at the three wet marks.
Ninsun: [quietly] And what does it want?

Narrator: She took a clean sheet of parchment and laid it over the altered map.

Zik: [skeptical] You just said the city might be writing through the map.
Ninsun: [calmly] Yes.
Narrator: Her stylus began moving.
Ninsun: [defiantly] That doesn't mean it gets to keep the only copy.
Narrator: She kept working.
Narrator: Outside, somewhere in the lower districts, another alley was probably opening while they slept. Or closing. Or deciding which names still deserved to remain uncrossed.
Narrator: Zeek left her to it and checked the linen on his left hand. The mark was warm. Not urgent. Just present.
Narrator: He did not open the journal.
Narrator: He had not opened it in four days.
[pause 1.2s]
Narrator: The decision still felt small. He was starting to trust that about it.
[pause 3s]


CHAPTER 11 — THE MARK THAT WASN’T OURS

Narrator: CHAPTER 11
[pause 1s]
Narrator: The Mark That Wasn't Ours
[pause 2s]
Narrator: They found the first forged notch on the eighth day.
Narrator: It was in a narrow cut between two dye-houses that had opened overnight and already started to close at the edges. Two clean marks sat at the base of the left wall, cut at the same depth and angle NEEN-soon used. Beside them someone had added a third—slightly deeper, the edges still sharp with stone dust.
Narrator: BAH-lahg saw it first. He stopped so suddenly that En-KHEH-doo nearly walked into his back.
Balag: [tense] That one's not ours.
Narrator: NEEN-soon crouched and studied the three marks without touching them. After a long moment she shook her head.
Ninsun: [intently] The third isn't mine. The angle's wrong. Whoever cut it was stronger in the wrist and less patient.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo exhaled through her nose.
Enhedu: [annoyed] So either we're not the only ones marking, or the fucking city learned the system.
Narrator: No one liked either version.
Narrator: Zeek ran his thumb along the third notch. The stone dust came away clean. Fresh. Maybe an hour old. Maybe less. He looked up the short passage. It ended in the usual blank wall that would probably be ordinary brick again by evening.
Zik: [firmly] We leave it,
Narrator: he said.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg frowned.
Balag: [skeptical] Just walk away?
Zik: [firmly] We mark our own and we move. If we start chasing every cut that isn't ours, we'll still be here when the streets forget our names.
Narrator: NEEN-soon carefully added the location to her ugly, incomplete map, noting the forged third mark with a small cross. Her handwriting had gotten smaller and tighter over the last few days, as if she were trying to fit more city onto less paper.
Narrator: They backed out. Behind them the passage had already narrowed enough that a man would have to turn sideways to enter. By the time they reached the main street it looked like a shallow service recess any local would have sworn had always been there.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: That night the consequences arrived.
Narrator: A boy from the lower market found them at the cheap room they were using. He couldn't have been more than fourteen, all elbows and caution. He held out a folded scrap of parchment like it might bite.
Messenger Boy: [nervous] Man said to give this to the ones cutting marks on the walls,
Narrator: he said.
Messenger Boy: [nervous] Said you'd know.
Narrator: BAH-lahg crouched until he was eye level with the boy.
Balag: [gruffly] What man?
Narrator: The boy shrugged.
Messenger Boy: [sullen] Didn't say.
Balag: [patiently] What did he look like?
Narrator: Another shrug.
Messenger Boy: [sullen] Old.
Balag: [patiently] How old?
Narrator: The boy frowned as though the question itself was unreasonable.
Messenger Boy: [sullen] Older than me.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo snorted.
Enhedu: [snorts] Helpful.
Narrator: BAH-lahg reached into his pouch and produced a small bronze coin.
Balag: [dryly] Try again.
Narrator: The boy looked at the coin.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Then at BAH-lahg.
Messenger Boy: [eager] He had clean hands.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Silence.
Narrator: BAH-lahg lowered the coin.
Balag: [surprised] What?
Messenger Boy: [matter-of-fact] That's what I noticed.
Narrator: The boy scratched behind one ear.
Messenger Boy: [casually] Everyone around here has dirty hands.
Narrator: He held up his own fingers.
Narrator: The nails were packed with dust.
Messenger Boy: [quietly] The man didn't.
Narrator: NEEN-soon looked up from the table.
Ninsun: [thoughtful] A scribe.
Messenger Boy: [unsure] Maybe.
Narrator: The boy shook his head.
Messenger Boy: [unsure] He didn't walk like one.
Ninsun: [curious] Temple?
Messenger Boy: [frustrated] I don't know.
Ninsun: [curious] Merchant?
Messenger Boy: [frustrated] I don't know.
Ninsun: [curious] Soldier?
Messenger Boy: [frustrated] I don't know.
Narrator: The boy looked genuinely frustrated now.
Messenger Boy: [frustrated] He stood where the alley bends.
Ninsun: [uneasy] Waiting?
Messenger Boy: [uneasy] Like he already knew I'd come.
Narrator: BAH-lahg exchanged a glance with Zeek.
Balag: [tense] Did he watch us?
Narrator: The boy nodded immediately.
Messenger Boy: [uneasy] He knew which room.
Narrator: Zeek's expression didn't change.
Zik: [sharply] Did you tell him?
Messenger Boy: [nervous] No.
Zik: [suspicious] He guessed?
Messenger Boy: [nervous] He pointed.
Zik: [sharply] Before you came upstairs?
Messenger Boy: [nervous] Before I crossed the street.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Nobody spoke.
Narrator: The boy finally accepted the coin.
Messenger Boy: [hesitant] He also said something else.
Narrator: BAH-lahg stiffened.
Balag: [tense] What?
Narrator: The boy frowned, trying to remember.
Messenger Boy: [hesitant] 'Tell them…'
Narrator: He looked at the ceiling.
Messenger Boy: [ominous] '…they've started making the wrong marks.'
Narrator: He shrugged.
Messenger Boy: [casually] Didn't make much sense.
Narrator: He hurried down the stairs before anyone could stop him.
Narrator: BAH-lahg remained at the doorway until the footsteps disappeared.
Narrator: Then he quietly slid the bolt across the door.
Narrator: He didn't wait for payment. He was already gone when BAH-lahg stepped into the lane to look for him.
Narrator: Zeek unfolded the scrap.
Narrator: One line, written in a neat, anonymous hand:
Narrator: [slowly] STOP MARKING WHAT DOES NOT BELONG TO YOU.
Narrator: No signature. No threat beyond the words themselves. The parchment was the same cheap stock the lower scribes used for temporary notes.
Narrator: Zeek turned the parchment over.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Blank.
Narrator: He held it toward the lamp.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Nothing showed through.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo reached for it.
Enhedu: [intently] The handwriting.
Zik: [curious] What about it?
Enhedu: [thoughtful] It isn't hurried.
Narrator: She studied the strokes.
Enhedu: [uneasy] Whoever wrote this expected we'd read it.
Narrator: BAH-lahg frowned.
Balag: [skeptical] People usually expect that from letters.
Enhedu: [firmly] No.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo tapped one corner.
Enhedu: [ominous] They expect we'll understand.
Narrator: NEEN-soon stood and crossed the room.
Narrator: She took the parchment without asking.
Narrator: For nearly a minute she said nothing.
Narrator: Then she reached into her satchel and removed the map.
Narrator: She laid the warning beside it.
Narrator: Zeek watched her eyes move between the two.
Ninsun: [intently] The spacing.
Balag: [impatient] What spacing?
Narrator: BAH-lahg asked.
Ninsun: [intently] The margins.
Narrator: She pointed.
Ninsun: [uneasy] The left margin matches mine.
Balag: [impatient] So?
Ninsun: [uneasy] So does the line height.
Narrator: BAH-lahg blinked.
Balag: [grumbling] I feel like scholars are inventing reasons to be frightened.
Narrator: NEEN-soon ignored him.
Ninsun: [intense] I leave exactly this much room at the top of every page.
Narrator: She demonstrated with her finger.
Ninsun: [uneasy] So does whoever wrote this.
Balag: [dismissively] Lots of people do.
Ninsun: [firmly] No.
Narrator: She looked up.
Ninsun: [uneasy] Not by accident.
Narrator: The room grew very quiet.
Narrator: Zeek picked up the note again.
Zik: [quietly] You think someone copied your habits.
Ninsun: [hesitant] I think…
Narrator: She hesitated.
Ninsun: [hesitant] …someone has seen enough of my work to imitate it.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo folded her arms.
Enhedu: [shrewdly] Or you've seen theirs.
Narrator: NEEN-soon didn't answer.
Narrator: That silence worried Zeek more than anything she could have said.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo read it over his shoulder.
Enhedu: [dryly] Someone's territorial.
Balag: [grimly] Or the city hired better messengers,
Narrator: BAH-lahg said.
Narrator: NEEN-soon took the scrap and set it beside her map without comment. After a moment she drew a small, precise box around the entire western dye-house district and wrote a single word inside it:
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: [slowly] CLAIMED.
Narrator: Zeek watched her work. The journal was still in his pack. Four days had become five. He had not opened it. The decision still felt like the only part of this place he actually controlled.
Narrator: He flexed his left hand inside the linen. The mark was quiet.
Narrator: Outside, somewhere in the lower districts, another passage was probably opening. Or closing. Or deciding which marks it would allow to remain.
Narrator: They had drawn a line on the city.
Narrator: Somewhere, someone had noticed.
Narrator: Whether that someone lived above the streets or beneath them, none of the four could yet say.
Narrator: But for the first time since opening the tomb, they no longer felt like explorers.
[pause 1.2s]
Narrator: They felt observed.
[pause 3s]


CHAPTER 12 — THE PRICE OF THE MARK

Narrator: CHAPTER 12
[pause 1s]
Narrator: The Price of the Mark
[pause 2s]
Narrator: The first real cost arrived two days after the warning note.
Narrator: They had been working a narrow cut behind the tanners' row—two notches of their own, no forged third, nothing that should have drawn attention. The passage was short and already starting to close when BAH-lahg's spear found something soft under the dust at the far end.
Narrator: He stopped. Everyone stopped.
Narrator: It was a pack. Ordinary. Canvas and leather, the kind any lower-district porter carried. The straps had been cut clean through. Inside was a water skin, a half-eaten loaf gone hard, and a small clay tablet wrapped in cloth. No name. No identifying marks. Just the ordinary gear of someone who had expected to walk out again.
Narrator: NEEN-soon unwrapped the tablet. The writing was hurried and already fading at the edges, as if the clay itself had decided the words were temporary.
Narrator: Three lines:
Narrator: [slowly] THEY ARE CUTTING THE WALLS
Narrator: [slowly] THE CITY DOES NOT LIKE WITNESSES
Narrator: [slowly] I SHOULD NOT HAVE FOLLOWED THE MARKS
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo exhaled slowly.
Enhedu: [grimly] So we're not the only ones.
Ninsun: [flatly] We were never the only ones,
Narrator: NEEN-soon said. Her voice had gone flat.
Ninsun: [flatly] We were just the ones still walking.
Narrator: BAH-lahg turned the empty pack over with the butt of his spear.
Balag: [grimly] No blood. No body. Just the gear.
Narrator: Zeek crouched beside the pack instead of answering immediately.
Narrator: The canvas had rotted at the corners but not enough to explain its condition. One strap had been sliced cleanly through. The other looked as though someone had started to cut it before changing their mind.
Narrator: He lifted the water skin.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Still damp.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg noticed.
Balag: [uneasy] That's impossible.
Zik: [quietly] It shouldn't be.
Balag: [tense] How long has this passage been here?
Zik: [thoughtful] This one?
Narrator: Zeek looked around the chamber.
Zik: [quietly] Hours.
Balag: [uneasy] And the water?
Narrator: He uncorked the skin.
Narrator: The smell was fresh.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not stagnant.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not sour.
Narrator: Fresh enough to drink.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo frowned.
Enhedu: [uneasy] So whoever owned this…
Zik: [quietly] …was here recently.
Narrator: NEEN-soon looked up from the tablet.
Ninsun: [ominous] Or recently enough that the city hasn't decided otherwise.
Narrator: Zeek searched the pack more carefully.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: A bronze needle.
Narrator: Two fishing hooks wrapped in cloth.
Narrator: A length of twine.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Nothing valuable.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Nothing worth stealing.
Narrator: Then his fingers touched something hidden inside the lining.
Narrator: He carefully split the loose stitching.
Narrator: A tiny clay token fell into his palm.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Round.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Unfired.
Narrator: Pressed with a single seal.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not the spiral-drop.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: A simple reed.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo leaned closer.
Enhedu: [curious] Meaning?
Zik: [quietly] I don't know.
Narrator: NEEN-soon took the token.
Ninsun: [intently] It isn't a merchant's mark.
Enhedu: [curious] Temple?
Ninsun: [quietly] No.
Narrator: She turned it over.
Ninsun: [softly] It looks personal.
Narrator: BAH-lahg scratched his beard.
Balag: [thoughtful] So somebody expected to come back.
Narrator: Zeek looked at the carefully hidden token.
Zik: [quietly] No.
Narrator: He folded the lining back into place.
Zik: [grimly] They expected someone else to.
Narrator: Nobody liked that answer.
Zik: [grimly] That's worse,
Narrator: Zeek said.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: No one argued.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: They took the tablet. They left the pack where it was. On the way out NEEN-soon cut a fourth notch beside their original two—deeper, angrier, the kind of mark that said this place had already collected its price. Zeek didn't stop her.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: That night the cheap room felt smaller.
Narrator: No one unpacked immediately.
Narrator: BAH-lahg leaned his spear against the wall but kept looking toward the door as though expecting another messenger to appear.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo poured water into four cups.
Narrator: Only three were touched.
Narrator: The fourth sat untouched beside the abandoned pack.
Narrator: After several minutes BAH-lahg pointed at it.
Balag: [gruffly] Who's that for?
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo looked down.
Narrator: She hadn't realized she'd poured it.
Narrator: Without answering, she emptied the cup back into the jar.
Narrator: No one mentioned it again.
Narrator: NEEN-soon finally broke the silence.
Ninsun: [softly] I'm trying to remember every name I've ever found scratched out.
Zik: [gently] Why?
Narrator: Zeek asked.
Ninsun: [sadly] What if one of them belonged to him?

Ninsun: [quietly] He followed marks.
Narrator: She touched the tablet gently.
Ninsun: [softly] He wrote this.

Balag: [gently] You think he was like us.
Ninsun: [softly] I think…
Narrator: She hesitated.
Ninsun: [hesitant] …he thought he was the first.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Silence settled over the room.
Narrator: Zeek understood the thought immediately.
Narrator: Every discovery they had made.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Every notch.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Every map.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Every theory.
Narrator: Someone else might have reached exactly the same conclusions.
Narrator: And disappeared before leaving more than three hurried lines.
Narrator: NEEN-soon sat on the floor with her map spread wide, the warning note, the uncrossed name, and the new tablet arranged in a careful line beside it. She had drawn a second box around the tanners' row and written a single word inside:
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: [slowly] COST.
Narrator: BAH-lahg cleaned his spear in silence. En-KHEH-doo turned the small copper figurine between her fingers and said nothing. Zeek sat with his back against the wall and did not open the journal. Six days now. The decision still felt like the only clean thing he owned in this place.
Narrator: After a long time En-KHEH-doo spoke.
Enhedu: [flatly] We keep marking, someone else pays. We stop marking, we walk blind. That's the math.
Narrator: BAH-lahg grunted.
Balag: [grunts] Same fucking math as always. Just higher numbers.
Narrator: NEEN-soon didn't look up from the map.
Ninsun: [determined] I'm not stopping.
Zik: [quietly] Didn't think you were,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Narrator: He flexed his left hand inside the linen. The mark was warm. Not demanding. Just present, like a second pulse that had decided it belonged there.
Narrator: Outside, the lower districts continued their ordinary noise—arguments, animals, the constant low pressure of a city that never fully slept. Somewhere in those streets another passage was probably opening. Or closing. Or waiting for the next set of marks to decide whether the people who cut them would be allowed to walk back out.
Narrator: They had drawn a line.
Narrator: The city had answered.
Narrator: Now someone else had paid the first real price for it.
Narrator: Zeek lay against the wall with the journal still sealed inside his pack.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Six days.
Narrator: Every hour he refused to open it felt like a choice he had made himself.
Narrator: Every shifted street made that choice feel smaller.
Narrator: Somewhere beneath the city, another explorer had vanished badly enough to leave his food behind, his water unfinished, and his warning still waiting to be read.
Narrator: Zeek wondered how many others had done the same.
Narrator: He wondered how many maps had been started.
Narrator: How many journals had remained closed.
Narrator: How many people had believed they were discovering the First City.
Narrator: Outside, somewhere below the ordinary streets of OO-rook, stone answered stone with a single distant knock.
[pause 1.2s]
Narrator: This time, nobody in the room pretended they hadn't heard it.
[pause 3s]


CHAPTER 13 — THE THIRD NOTCH

Narrator: CHAPTER 13
[pause 1s]
Narrator: The Third Notch
[pause 2s]
Narrator: The passage opened in the middle of the afternoon while they were still arguing about whether to take a paying survey job on the east side.
Narrator: One moment the wall behind the tanners' drying racks was ordinary brick. The next it had a dark mouth in it, already breathing cooler air. Three notches sat at the base of the left wall—two of theirs from two days earlier, and a third cut deeper, the edges still sharp with fresh stone dust.
Narrator: BAH-lahg stopped so hard the nearest donkey shied.
Balag: [tense] That's not ours.
Narrator: NEEN-soon was already moving toward it.
Narrator: BAH-lahg stepped in front of her. Not aggressive. Just solid.
Balag: [firmly] No.
Narrator: She looked at him.
Ninsun: [defiantly] It's three. We don't know who cut the third.
Narrator: BAH-lahg didn't answer immediately.
Narrator: He looked past her into the passage.
Narrator: The cold air spilling from it carried the familiar smell of damp clay.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Nothing moved.
Narrator: That somehow made it worse.
Balag: [firmly] You remember the pack.
Ninsun: [quietly] I remember.
Balag: [firmly] You remember the basin.
Ninsun: [quietly] I remember.
Balag: [firmly] You remember the room that learned your name.
Narrator: She looked away.
Ninsun: [quietly] I remember.
Narrator: BAH-lahg rested both hands on the butt of his spear.
Balag: [pleading] Then stop making me say the rest.
Narrator: NEEN-soon folded her arms.
Ninsun: [intense] If we'd turned away from the first passage, we'd still think this city was ordinary.
Balag: [firmly] If we'd walked farther into the basin room, you wouldn't have come back.
Ninsun: [defiantly] You don't know that.
Balag: No.
Narrator: His voice stayed calm.
Balag: [calmly] I know we almost didn't.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo watched them without interrupting.
Narrator: She had seen the argument before.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not the words.
Narrator: The shape of it.
Narrator: One person believing every answer lived one step farther.
Narrator: Another believing survival depended on knowing when to stop walking.
Narrator: Zeek realized they were both right.
Narrator: That was the problem.
Balag: [firmly] That's exactly why we're not going in.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo had come up on Zeek's left. She studied the notches, then the narrowing edges of the passage, then the way the light from the street already seemed reluctant to enter.
Enhedu: [urgently] It's starting to close. Whatever's in there doesn't plan on staying open long.
Narrator: NEEN-soon's voice stayed level, but the stylus in her hand had gone white at the knuckles.
Ninsun: [intense] If we walk away every time the city answers, the only record left is the one it keeps. That's how names disappear. That's how my sister disappeared.
Narrator: BAH-lahg didn't move.
Balag: [angrily] And if we walk in every time it answers, we end up like the pack with the straps cut. I'm not carrying another empty bag back to this fucking room.
Narrator: The passage breathed once, soft and patient. The third notch looked deeper in the shifting light.
Narrator: Zeek felt the mark under the linen give a single slow pulse. He ignored it.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo glanced at him.
Enhedu: [impatient] You're the one who decides the jobs. So decide.
Narrator: The street behind them kept up its ordinary noise—drying racks flapping, someone cursing a late delivery, a child crying over nothing important. The four of them stood in the small pocket of quieter air the open passage had made, and for a moment no one added anything else.
Narrator: Zeek looked at the three notches. At NEEN-soon's face. At the way BAH-lahg's shoulders had set. At En-KHEH-doo already calculating whether the risk had a price worth naming.
Zik: [firmly] We don't go in,
Narrator: he said.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: NEEN-soon's mouth tightened.
Zik: [firmly] We don't go in,
Narrator: he repeated,
Zik: [firmly] and we change the rules right now. No more following marks we didn't cut. No more open cuts in districts we haven't walked twice. If a passage already has three notches, we leave it. And if we find another pack, we leave the tablet where it is and we walk.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Silence held for two breaths.
Narrator: Then BAH-lahg nodded once, short and final.
Balag: [approvingly] That's not stopping.
Zik: [dryly] No,
Narrator: Zeek said.
Zik: [dryly] It's refusing to get stupid about it.
Narrator: NEEN-soon stared at the third notch a moment longer. When she finally stepped back, the motion looked like it cost her. She pulled her map free, found the tanners' row, and wrote a single word beside the passage in the same tight hand she used for everything that mattered:
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: [slowly] LIMIT.
Narrator: She stared at the word for several moments.
Narrator: Then she crossed it out.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg frowned.
Balag: [skeptical] Changed your mind?
Ninsun: [quietly] No.
Narrator: She wrote again.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not LIMIT.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: [slowly] RETURN LATER.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo looked over her shoulder.
Enhedu: [skeptical] That's not the same thing.
Ninsun: [firmly] No.
Narrator: NEEN-soon capped the ink.
Ninsun: [precisely] A limit means we're afraid.
Narrator: She folded the map.
Ninsun: [satisfied] A return means we're patient.
Narrator: BAH-lahg snorted.
Balag: [snorts] You found the scholar's way to lose the argument.
Ninsun: [primly] I found the accurate one.
Narrator: Zeek smiled despite himself.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Only slightly.
Zik: [amused] Leave it.
Narrator: She did.
Narrator: En-KHEH-doo exhaled through her nose.
Enhedu: [dryly] I'll take the limited version of dead over the ambitious one.
Narrator: The passage had already narrowed.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Not loud.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Stone settling.
[pause 0.6s]
Narrator: Then another.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: BAH-lahg stopped.
Narrator: The four of them turned together.
Narrator: The shallow recess remained.
[pause 1.5s]
Narrator: Nothing moved.
Narrator: Then the wall bulged outward.
[pause 0.5s]
Narrator: Just once.
Narrator: As though someone on the other side had placed a hand against it.
[pause 0.5s]

[pause 0.5s]

Narrator: BAH-lahg answered immediately.
